1. The black nodes are A and J.
2. Node A directly connects to nodes E, B, and S.
3. Node J directly connects to nodes G, I, and O.
4. We proceed iteratively by exploring the neighbors of the neighbors:

- From node A:
  - A -> E
  - A -> B (and then B connects to F, T, S, and E)
  - A -> S (and then S connects to E and B)

- From node J:
  - J -> G (and then G connects to C, N, R, O, and H)
  - J -> I (and then I connects to H)
  - J -> O (and then O connects to G, Q, and R)

5. Continuing the search:
   - From node B:
     - B -> F (and then F connects to T)
     - B -> T

   - From node G:
     - G -> C (and then C connects to N)
     - G -> R (and then R connects to O)

- After exploring more, we find that:
  - A -> B -> G -> J. This path includes nodes A, B, G, and J.

The path between A and J in this case must involve nodes G and I (as they are connected to J) or involve some intermediary nodes from the connections of G, I, or O.

Given that the shortest identifiable path is A -> B -> G -> J.

Thus, the shortest path length is 3 and the path is A to B to G to J.

Length: 3, Path: [A, B, G, J]

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - Node A
   - Node J

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of Node A:**
   - A is connected to: E, S, F, and B

3. **Find the immediate neighbors of Node J:**
   - J is connected to: G, O, Q, and I

4. **Trace paths from Node A to Node J through intermediate nodes:**
   - From A, we consider its neighbors: E, S, F, and B.
   - Next, consider the neighbors of these nodes and continue this process until we find a path to J.

5. **Path from A to J:**
   - A → B → C → G → J
     - A connects to B
     - B connects to C
     - C connects to G
     - G connects to J

This path passes through 4 edges (A → B → C → G → J), making it a path of length 4.

6. **Check for any shorter paths:**
   - No shorter paths are found by tracing through other intermediate nodes.

Thus, the shortest path from A to J is:

Length: 4, Path: [A, B, C, G, J]

To determine the minimum number of nodes that need to be removed so that the two black nodes (E and T) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths connecting these two nodes.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:** E and T.
2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of E and T:**
   - E is connected to: A, B, F, S.
   - T is connected to: B, G, S.
3. **Identify the common neighboring nodes:** B and S.
4. **Find all paths between E and T:**
   - Path 1: E -> B -> T
   - Path 2: E -> S -> T

To disconnect E and T, we need to remove nodes that are part of all paths connecting them. In this case, removing either B or S would disrupt both paths.

However, since we need the minimum number of nodes to be removed, we should check if there are other nodes that can be removed to achieve the same result.

5. **Analyze further connections:**
   - Removing B or S individually will disconnect E and T.
   - Removing B: E -> S -> T is still connected.
   - Removing S: E -> B -> T is still connected.

Thus, removing either B or S alone is not sufficient. We need to remove both B and S to ensure E and T are completely disconnected.

However, there is another path to consider:
   - Path 3: E -> F -> C -> G -> T

To disconnect E and T completely, we need to remove nodes that are part of all paths connecting them. Removing B and S will not disrupt this path. Therefore, we need to remove additional nodes to disrupt this path.

6. **Identify additional nodes to remove:**
   - Removing F or G will disrupt the path E -> F -> C -> G -> T.
